key: S021\n  name: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\n  description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: Sometimes when my supervisor asks me a simple question about which floors I finished, I start talking about the old bus route, the garden plot, and the church basement, and I can hear myself not getting back to the answer.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There have been times at work when I keep wiping the same patch of glass over and over or stand in the supply closet holding the broom because it feels like I have to wait for the right sign before I can move on.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: What brought me in is I don’t feel like myself anymore—I barely talk to people, I just go flat and quiet, and even getting started on the cleaning route feels like pushing a loaded cart uphill.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, doing crosswords with coffee, or showing the younger guys tricks for stripping a floor, but now I mostly let those things sit because they don’t give me anything back.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Most days food just tastes like cardboard, so I skip meals or eat a few crackers after my shift, and my work pants have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even if I sleep through the night, my arms and legs feel heavy by the time I get to the building, and small jobs like hauling trash bags wear me out more than they should.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I failed everybody by losing my old place and not keeping my life together, like if the church pantry is short on supplies or a hallway gets missed, somehow that’s on me too.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have some big plan, but a lot of mornings I wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up at all, and that scares me enough to say it out loud.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was at my lowest—barely eating, feeling guilty, and thinking I might not want to wake up—I was also hearing that man’s voice at work and believing people in the building were watching me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n It's just... hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to... I liked fishing on Saturdays. Or just sitting with a crossword and a cup of coffee. Even at work, I used to like showing the new guys how to strip a floor right. Now... I don't know. I just let it all sit. It doesn't... none of it gives me anything back anymore. So I just don't do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any pleasure when you do things, even small things, like eating or taking a warm shower?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I just... I eat because I'm supposed to. Showers are just... another thing I have to do. It's all just the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think things will ever get better for you?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I haven't really thought about it. It just is what it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not like that. I don't have some... big plan or anything. \n\nIt's just... I don't know. A lot of mornings, I wake up and just think... it might be easier if I didn't wake up at all. I guess... well, it scares me enough to actually say it out loud to you right now. But no, I haven't tried to do anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don't know... a while, I guess. Maybe... maybe almost a year and a half? It just kind of started creeping in... around the time I had to move. Left my old apartment, my old neighborhood... it's been about a year and five months since then. Just been... building up since that happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I just do my job, clean the buildings, come home. I haven't really noticed moving any different. I don't really eat much anymore. Most days, food just tastes like cardboard. So I end up skipping meals... maybe just have a few crackers when I get home from my shift. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess so. I just... I think about how I lost my old place. How I just couldn't keep things together. It feels like... like I failed everybody. Even things that aren't mine to fix, I just... if the church pantry doesn't have enough food, or if I miss a spot in one of the hallways at work... I don't know. It just feels like it's my fault somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. It's... kind of like what I just was saying. I just feel like I'm not good for much. Because I couldn't keep my life together... losing my place and all. I just... I feel like a failure to everybody. Even with the church pantry or... or missing spots at work. I don't know. Just useless, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. Sometimes, maybe. When I'm mopping after hours... I'll hear a man's voice. He'll say my name, or... tell me I missed a spot. It sounds real clear. Like he's just standing right over by the stairwell. But then I look, and... there's nobody there. Just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're at the worst point you've ever been in your life right now?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know about that. I mean... things aren't good right now, obviously. It's just... hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to... I liked fishing on Saturdays. Or just sitting with a crossword and a cup of coffee. Even at work, I used to like showing the new guys how to strip a floor right. Now... I don't know. I just let it all sit. It doesn't... none of it gives me anything back anymore. So I just don't do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any pleasure when you do things, even small things, like eating or taking a warm shower?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I just... I eat because I'm supposed to. Showers are just... another thing I have to do. It's all just the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think things will ever get better for you?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I haven't really thought about it. It just is what it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not like that. I don't have some... big plan or anything. \n\nIt's just... I don't know. A lot of mornings, I wake up and just think... it might be easier if I didn't wake up at all. I guess... well, it scares me enough to actually say it out loud to you right now. But no, I haven't tried to do anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don't know... a while, I guess. Maybe... maybe almost a year and a half? It just kind of started creeping in... around the time I had to move. Left my old apartment, my old neighborhood... it's been about a year and five months since then. Just been... building up since that happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I just do my job, clean the buildings, come home. I haven't really noticed moving any different. I don't really eat much anymore. Most days, food just tastes like cardboard. So I end up skipping meals... maybe just have a few crackers when I get home from my shift. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess so. I just... I think about how I lost my old place. How I just couldn't keep things together. It feels like... like I failed everybody. Even things that aren't mine to fix, I just... if the church pantry doesn't have enough food, or if I miss a spot in one of the hallways at work... I don't know. It just feels like it's my fault somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. It's... kind of like what I just was saying. I'll hear a man's voice. He'll say my name, or... tell me I missed a spot. It sounds real clear. Like he's just standing right over by the stairwell. But then I look, and... there's nobody there. Just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're at the worst point you've ever been in your life right now?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know about that. I mean... things aren't good right now, obviously. I mean... I still have to go in to work, so... I don't stay in bed all day. Just... drag myself out, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other experiences like hearing voices when you're alone, besides the man's voice you mentioned earlier?\n\nPatient: ...No. Just that one.
